ALL SOFTWARE TEST RESULTS - COMPRESSION RATIO AND SPEED FOR EVERY TOOL TESTED
Author: Michael Hugh Harrington          Date: 6 November 2026
HX is Patent Pending (U.S. App. 64/192,843)
Copyright (c) 2026 Michael Hugh Harrington. HX, HX Fast, HX Complete, the cascade and the per-slice
build: all rights reserved (not MIT). Our other tools: MIT. Third-party tools keep their licences.
====================================================================================================
Built only from results already recorded (no new benchmarks were run for this document).

HOW TO READ
  Ratio = original bytes / compressed bytes (higher is better), written x.xx×. Speeds are given
  both as seconds (as recorded) and as MB/s, where MB = 1,000,000 bytes of ORIGINAL data per
  second (original size / recorded time). "n/r" = not recorded. A "~" before a byte count means
  the source recorded only a size relative to the original; the ratio is exact, the byte count is
  derived from it and the known original size (rounding error under 0.0001%). Every listed result
  decoded back exactly unless a note says otherwise.
  Group column g: blank = original third-party program, * = our program, m = modified tool (theirs
  plus our changes; the stock decoder reads it), r = mutant or research build (needs our decoder,
  uses a research-only part, or is a test control).
  Machines: "cloud" = 2-core cloud machine (busy, noisy times); "desktop" = Michael's Ryzen 5
  7500X3D, 6 cores / 12 threads. Compare speeds only inside one table (same machine, same
  session).
  Section order: 1 tool list by group; 2 results by corpus; 3 HX; 4 modified tools; 5 mutants and
  research builds; 6 task-level tests; 7 our programs outside the corpus tables; 8 cmix / paq8px;
  9 final rankings; 10 gaps.

====================================================================================================
3. HX - ALL HX FAST AND HX COMPLETE RESULTS
====================================================================================================
What they are. HX splits a file into independent segments of about 1 MB, each decoded on its own
core; for every segment it tries the Harrington Exchange lines (byte lanes, 12-bit samples,
context lines, audio predictors), Harrington's Sets and the cascade's repeat line, and keeps the
smallest. HX Fast is the speed-first mode. HX Complete adds whole-file and split routes with BSC,
bzip3, LZMA (BCJ2 for x86), PPMd and Brotli, the x86 filter, and a learned router. The per-slice
build (routing slice by slice) is under construction and has no results yet. Source-file build
labels: hxf 8 / 9 / 11 = HX Fast builds, hxc / 11 / 12 / 13b = HX Complete builds, hx15 = HX
Complete with the learned router, hx / hxs = the HX lines bench (entbench).

3.6 HX LINES vs THE ENTROPY CODERS - BIASED DATA WITH A KNOWN FLOOR (desktop, 128 MB files, in
      memory)
  source: HU-pretrain-results\biased-desktop\Biased-Data-vs-Entropy-Coders-2026-11-03.txt
  source                          floor | best coder       ratio   MB/s | HX lines  MB/s 1 / 12 thr
  E 16-bit, high byte biased      1.60× | Huff0            1.35×  1,915 |    1.60×    2,048 / 9,804
  F 12-bit noise, packed          1.88× | FSE              1.32×    865 |    1.88×      880 / 6,328
  G bits with memory (Markov)     2.76× | rANS             2.63×    223 |    2.75×      172 / 1,571
  D bias changes every 64 KB      1.63× | adaptive arith   1.60×     23 |    1.62×      336 / 1,800
  Floor = the exact entropy of the source, shown as the best possible ratio. D uses HX lines +
  Sets (its speeds are recorded as ~336 MB/s on 1 thread and ~1,800 MB/s on 12). Plain biased bits
  and Zipf bytes (A, B, C) tie the best coder on the floor. NIST ringOsc (1 MB, cloud): HX lines
  12.56×, decode 4.4 ms, vs best coder FSE 8.00×; BSC 12.74× (71 ms) and bzip3 12.72× (102 ms).

====================================================================================================
4. MODIFIED TOOLS (-hi tools) - stock decoders read every output
====================================================================================================
  Full corpus rows for these tools are in section 2 (Silesia LZMA/7-Zip/RAR race, Canterbury
  tables, Silesia 1 MB sample, Goodwin synthetic, Mahoney race, x-ray, mr, kennedy.xls). Results
  recorded only outside those tables:

  xz-hi: Canterbury 5.70× -> 6.07×; decode 32.1 -> 24.8 ms; Goodwin G_audio16 439,256 -> 382,084 B
  (decode 23.1 -> 18.1 ms); modern data 12 files 4,053,776 -> 4,040,884 B, decode 389 -> 360 ms.

  bzip2-hi: Canterbury 5.18× -> 5.28×, decode 59.7 -> 55.4 ms; compress much slower (plrabn12 0.07
  -> 22.9 s).

  lzma-hi: Canterbury 5.70× -> 5.78×, 28.65 -> 28.55 ms; nci 19.27× -> 23.24× and 1.22x faster
  decode.

  7z-hi: Canterbury 5.68× -> 6.05×; ooffice 2.54× -> 3.04× (1.25x faster, BCJ2); nci 19.27× ->
  23.23× (1.20x faster); mr 3.63× -> 3.85×; x-ray 1.89× -> 2.02×.

  zstd-hi / Brotli-hi: small no-pain gains only (Canterbury table, section 2); zstd-hi counties
  export compress 72.9 -> 36.3 s.

  jxl-hi (stock cjxl effort 7, other settings searched): x-ray: +0.3% size with 5.3x faster
  decode, or 2.3% smaller with 1.35x faster; RG1 +2.9% / 7.5x; MR2 +3.6% / 4.8x; RG3 0.5% smaller
  / 1.30x; OBXXXX1A +4.8% / 1.37x. Lost on 693, MR-SIEMENS, ALOKA, eCT.

  JPEG-LS tuned (CharLS): x-ray 3,591,153 -> 3,574,443 B (2.36× -> 2.37×), decode 103.6 -> 87.6 ms
  (cloud rival3 run: 0.120 -> 0.100 s).

  flac-hi (tuned FLAC): 6 speech WAVs: flac -8 1.88× -> 1.92×, decode 136 -> 123 ms; later run
  1.93×, decode 0.171 s.

  RAR no-pain search: Silesia 3.99× -> 4.06×, decode 1,059 -> 1,019 ms; sao 1.31× -> 1.47× with
  -mcd-, decode 68.0 -> 61.5 ms.

  zpaq-hi: Canterbury 361,792 -> 359,424 B (one file per archive), compress 11.5 -> 20.9 s, decode
  11.8 -> 14.1 s (size win only); Calgary 716,354 B, Canterbury large 2,151,206 B (smallest of all
  there), GCB 9,137,324 B, enwik9 168,215,145 B (section 2, Mahoney race). Same-block multi-core:
  0.1-1.1% smaller than stock, decodes 1-37% slower.

  PDF stream rewrite: 139 pdf.js PDFs: originals 1.00×, qpdf known optimisation 1.17×, ours 1.19×;
  render 11.22 / 11.35 / 11.60 s; strict no-pain pick 1.18× at 11.20 s render.

  WebP / PNG searches: WebP lossless -z 9 on 24 Kodak images: 2.53× -> 2.53× (no real gain); PNG
  per-image filter + zlib strategy 1.79× -> 1.82×, decode 225 -> 202 ms (known optipng work, not
  counted).

  source for this section: memory /areas/hcm-benchmarks.md and /areas/combary.md; PC folders xz-hi,
      bzip2-hi,
  zpaq-hi, tools\*, verification 2026-10-28, HCM_Wins_Under_Standard.txt.

====================================================================================================
5. MUTANTS AND RESEARCH BUILDS
====================================================================================================

  lpaq1-h (lpaq1 + HCM record detector and contexts): Canterbury 392,132 -> 359,786 B (7.17× ->
  7.81×), decode 4.21 -> 4.18 s; Silesia 1 MB sample 3,643,900 -> 3,559,695 B; Goodwin synthetic
  8,113,745 B = smallest of all 14 tools (compress 146.8 s, decode 25.6 s); GCB 9,121,594 B
  (2.14×, smallest in the Mahoney race). Parallel 2 MB blocks: reymont decode 6.7 -> 4.1 s, x-ray
  12.5 -> 6.5 s, dickens 12.4 -> 7.0 s at 1-6% size cost.

  HCM v3.7 (research, private): HCM 3.6.1 + two kanzi-style contexts + kanzi EXE filter: ooffice
  2,251,050 -> 1,857,184 B (3.31×); samba 3,355,530 -> 3,296,662 B; full Silesia max 41.74 MB
  (section 2 table).

  Sets reorder (setsort, private): Silesia xml HCM 3.7 max 348,363 -> 341,969 B (15.63×), decode
  4.91 -> 4.98 s, beats kanzi -l 9 342,833; 16 KB windows 338,588 B (15.79×). pcap 112,917,841 B:
  HCM 3.7 max 88,498,954 -> 80,616,598 B (1.40×), decode 117.4 -> 102.5 s; kanzi 88,755,437 ->
  80,692,125; Brotli 11 89,598,288 -> 81,206,385; zstd -9 91,398,395 -> 82,701,553; zstd -3
  92,254,442 -> 83,401,428; LZ4 HC -12 95,392,369 -> 87,638,425; bzip2 -9 92,613,792 ->
  87,080,870; gzip -9 94,023,309 -> 92,702,776; BSC 86,687,460 -> 82,881,374.

  cascade (cascading lines; zstd build v1.1 and HCM build v3, private): desktop: hi16_mix 1.14× ->
  1.89×, dup_random_chunks 1.37× -> 2.32× (beats zstd --long 2.32×), random stored raw 1.00×; 3.2
  GB tar 4.09× vs 4.09× (v1.1 now keeps the smaller). HCM cascade v3 (desktop, Sets fast off ->
  on): hi16 1.17× -> 1.93× (decode 1.76 -> 3.20 s), switch 1.76× -> 2.14× (4.65 -> 4.80 s), dup
  1.39× -> 2.57× (5.04 -> 8.01 s).

  record delta front ends (Mutated): kennedy.xls zstd -22 64,815 -> 26,398 B with 2.6x faster
  decode; x-ray zstd -19 5,129,823 -> 4,508,494 B, decode 24.6 -> 20.2 ms; zstd -1 x-ray 1.25× ->
  1.69×; Snappy kennedy 2.44× -> 7.94× (966 -> 717 us); LZFSE kennedy 7.56× -> 18.78× (1,064 ->
  767 us). Silesia totals: section 2.

  change map (formerly activity map): kennedy gzip -9 209,721 -> 35,558 B, decode 2,148 ->
  ~1,313-1,476 us; slower on lz4 (39,997 B, 739 -> ~1,000 us); Snappy x-ray 1.00× -> 1.64× but
  11.4 vs 1.1 ms decode.

  Harrington Exchange (swap + juggle) front ends: synthetic packed 12-bit noise 324,507 B (1.62×;
  best known method 1.42×), decode 5.1 ms (zstd 0.89, xz 19.2 ms); NIST biased bits 19,722 B vs xz
  21,772 B, decode 1.49 ms (xz 1.13 ms). On real images renaming hurts (x-ray 12-bit split-only
  342,496 B is best).

  Parquet / ORC with record columns: DuckDB Parquet zstd: kennedy 6.31× -> 14.98×; x-ray 1.04× ->
  1.60× (read 137 -> 102 ms); ORC zlib: x-ray 1.37× -> 1.80× (46.1 -> 25.6 ms); kennedy 3.88× ->
  19.87× (2.8 -> 1.8 ms).

  hcm-trit, HCM-Bin, hcmx: Canterbury table in section 2; kennedy HCM-Bin v1 16,763 B (ZPAQ
  16,822) decode 0.79 s vs ZPAQ 6.35 s; ptt5 27,623 B (ZPAQ 28,563) 0.26 s vs 3.29 s. Calgary hcmx
  max 707,392 B (4.60×), smallest in the Mahoney race.

  HCM parallel and Sets modes (v3.5 / v3.6): Silesia samba v3.4 3,400,668 B (62.9 / 16.8 s) ->
  v3.5 blocks 3,443,819 (59.2 / 13.9 s) -> v3.6 Sets 3,406,084 (42.5 / 9.7 s); mozilla v3.5
  13,412,925 (130.2 / 32.7 s). Decoder memory, Silesia desktop: single stream 86.8 s / 107 MB,
  blocks 16 MB 58.8 s / 230 MB, Sets 16 MB 47.3 s / 271 MB, Sets 4 MB 26.6 s / 549 MB.

  IBM-style splitting controls (US8782019, US9792350) vs Sets, HCM fast, cloud: samba Sets
  3,406,080 B (40.5 / 9.2 s) vs equal blocks 3,426,219 (62.3 / 9.4 s), IBM-A 3,536,707 (38.7 /
  10.5 s), IBM-B 3,475,209 (57.7 / 12.6 s); mozilla Sets 13,419,755 (103.2 / 28.8 s) vs IBM-A
  13,489,282 (94.4 / 24.9 s). Source ibm-comparison-2026-10-29\results.txt.

  paq8px / cmix labs: paq8px lite model set ~2x faster (kennedy 414 -> 181 s) for 9.28× vs 9.31×
  on Canterbury; cmix run in 2.5-4 GB instead of 32 GB with no size change on fields.c (2,087 B).

  mzip research builds (AGPL, research only): Silesia Sets + mzip 16 MB 4.84× decode 16.8 s vs
  mzip 4.92× 24.8 s (cloud); HX Complete + mzip 4.81× on 9 of 12 files. Section 2.

  sources: memory /areas/hcm-benchmarks.md, /areas/combary.md, /areas/harringtons-sets.md; PC
      sets-cascade-2026-11-03,
  setsort-reorder-2026-11-04, lpaq1-h, hcm37, hcm-v35/36, HCM-tests\cascade-2026-11-03,

====================================================================================================
7. OUR PROGRAMS - OTHER RECORDED RESULTS (desktop speed records and early versions)
====================================================================================================

  hs_zstd 1.0 (20 GB Silesia, desktop, 3 rounds): decode 10.71 / 19.23 / 10.50 s vs zstd 19.46 /
  28.72 / 28.51 s (safe claim 1.5-1.9x); compress 15.6 s vs zstd 68.2 s (1 core) / 18.8 s (-T0);
  3.180× vs 3.183×.

  hs_zstd 1.1 vs pzstd, 10 GB (desktop): decode pzstd 6.87 s vs hs_zstd 4 MB 4.00 s (1.72x).

  hs_zstd core scaling (4 GB Silesia, zstd -3, desktop): decode at 1/2/4/6/8/12 jobs: hs_zstd 4 MB
  7.52 / 4.87 / 3.24 / 2.71 / 2.55 / 2.43 s; pzstd 4.77 / 4.04 / 3.60 / 3.78 / 3.49 / 3.53 s.
  Compress hs_zstd 19.59 -> 2.89 s (6.8x), pzstd 15.58 -> 3.78 s (4.1x).

  hs_zstd 1.3 ablation (16 MB blocks): 4 types switching every 1 MB: 1.2 1.57×, plain blocks
  1.71×, 1.3 1.88×, zstd -3 --long 1.71×; dup random chunks: 1.00× / 1.31× / 1.37× / 2.31×;
  Silesia joined 3.20× / 3.20× / 3.20× / 3.23×.

  hs_wrap 1.5 + gzip, fair gzip baselines (4 GB Silesia, level 6, desktop): decode gzip 14.16 s,
  pigz 9.55-9.77 s, rapidgzip 2.88 s, bgzip -@12 3.36 s (2.99×), hs_wrap 1.5 Sets 4.02 s (3.11×),
  plain blocks 4.03 s. rapidgzip beats hs_wrap + gzip on decode.

  hs_wrap 1.4 memory (2 GB, desktop): 16 MB blocks + hold 14: decode 1.9 s at 276 MB; 8 MB + hold
  14: 2.1 s at 165 MB; gzip alone 6.0 s at 9 MB.

  HCM v3 / v2 speed: v3 fast 3.96x compress / 4.10x decompress vs v2; memory fast 42 MB, max 68
  MB, v2 121 MB.

  HCM v3.6 Windows, samba (desktop): single 6.35× (33.4 / 8.1 s); blocks 6.27× (24.0 / 6.3 s);
  Sets 16 MB 6.34× (15.2 / 4.0 s); Sets 4 MB 6.19× (7.5 / 2.1 s).

  Early Python HCM (200 KB Python docs text): context-balance coder 6.73× (29,725 B), ~27 s each
  way; restore point BWT route 5.65× (35,378 B, 0.9 s); ZPAQ -m5 7.12× (28,101 B, 1.0 s), bzip2
  5.92×, xz 5.40×.

  H-Deflate v1 (4 deflate-heavy sets, cloud): PNG 24 files 1.54× (dec 10.0 s) vs precomp route
  1.23×; PDF 40 files 1.69× (3.89 s) vs 1.66×; Office 177 files 2.06× (5.22 s) vs 1.94×; archives
  1.645× (15.3 s) vs 1.645× (21.9 s). Full tables in section 2.

  sources: memory /areas/combary.md and /areas/hcm-benchmarks.md; PC HCM-tests\zstd20-2026-10-31,
  pzstd-2026-10-31, hs-zstd-scaling-2026-11-01, hs-zstd-ablation-1.3, HCM-tests\gzfair-2026-11-01,
      gzip-mem-2026-10-31,
  hcm-v36-windows, h-deflate-2026-11-04\H-Deflate-Results-2026-11-04.txt.

8. cmix AND paq8px - Compared via existing metrics, not actually tested
====================================================================================================

  Silesia (211,938,580 B): published cmix v20 28,302,847 B = 7.49×. Our best measured: HCM v3.6
  fast on the desktop 42,661,041 B = 4.97×; HCM v3.7 max (research) 4.06× file order; HX Complete
  4.72× at 8.6 s decode. Compared via existing metrics, not actually tested.

  Silesia images: cmix mr 1,827,572 B (5.46×) vs HX 2,069,305 B (4.82×); cmix x-ray 3,503,868 B
  (2.42×) vs HX 3,651,990 B (2.32×). Compared via existing metrics, not actually tested.

  Random Turing machine data (19,552,881 B): predicted paq8px (2009) ~8.74 MB (2.24×) vs HX
  Complete 9,185,811 B (2.13×) and lpaq1 9,162,095 B (2.13×). Compared via existing metrics, not
  actually tested.

  Canterbury: cmix published 9.61× and paq8px v217 -8 9.31×; our best hcm-trit v2 max + dictionary
  8.45×, hcmx max 8.08×. Compared via existing metrics, not actually tested.

  Biased files with a known floor: HX is within 0.06-0.31% of the floor (0.74% on the repeats
  file), so cmix or paq8px can be at most that much smaller there. Compared via existing metrics,
  not actually tested.

  Recorded exception: paq8px -1 / -6 was run once inside the hs_wrap speed matrix on 3 MB heads of
  10 Silesia files (section 2, "Silesia 3 MB heads"), and the paq8px / cmix lab runs of 25 Oct
  (section 5). No full paq8px or cmix benchmark was run.

  sources: plans\All-Units-vs-All-Units-2026-11-04.txt section 12; HU-pretrain-results\published\;
      memory
  /areas/combary.md (paq8px / cmix lab, cmix dictionary).

  Showcase - exceptional results: HX Fast decodes x-ray at 642 MB/s on 12 threads (0.0132 s) at
  2.32×, smaller than mzip, bzip3, BSC, kanzi and ZPAQ -m5; HX Complete is the smallest of every
  tool run on Calgary, Canterbury, Canterbury large and our synthetic set in the 3-4 Nov campaign
  while decoding faster than the runner-up; hcmx max is the smallest on Calgary in the Mahoney
  race (4.60×), zpaq-hi on Canterbury large (5.19×), lpaq1-h on the GCB (2.14×) and on Goodwin's
  synthetic set; HCM v3.4 max is the smallest of 14 tools on Canterbury (8.02×, decode 3.4 s vs
  ZPAQ -m5 11.5 s); HCM v3.6.1 is the smallest on the seismic, bank and plant data (plant: Sets
  mode 9.27×, 19% smaller than xz -9); the Sets reorder takes the 113 MB packet capture to 1.40×,
  the smallest of every tool run on it; HX lines sit within 0.06-0.31% of the entropy floor on
  structured biased data at 2-10 GB/s.

====================================================================================================
9b. TYPE x SPEED-TIER MATRIX - HX vs the best other tool in every tier
====================================================================================================
  Cells: 29 data types x 10 speed tiers = 290 cells. Filled (at least one HX or rival result
  measured inside the tier): 159; not tested: 131. HX measured inside the tier: 62 cells; a rival
  measured inside the tier: 155; both: 58. Cells where HX only qualifies from a faster tier (rule
  3d) show its entry with "^" and count as contested only if a rival is in the tier. The type list
  is the one master_report.py produces now: 29 types (its 3 Nov output had 26, the source of the
  26 x 10 = 260 count). The tiers are the Compression Tier Standard v2.0 1-2-5 series
  (plans\Compression-Tier-Standard-v2.0.txt; master_report.py itself defines no tiers): M = decode
  time / LZ4 default decode time on the same set; T1 M<=2, T2 <=5, T3 <=10, T4 <=20, T5 <=50, T6
  <=100, T7 <=200, T8 <=500, T9 <=2,000, T10 >2,000.
  Rule used (as in Results-Summary-Kings-2026-11-04.txt): the HX entry is the smallest HX result
  whose M is in that tier or a faster one; the rival is the smallest original third-party tool
  whose M lands in that tier. Only full-coverage exact results count; HX research combinations
  (precomp, mzip, x264 routes) are left out. Data: the 3-4 Nov cloud campaign (section 2), the
  same data behind the HX-vs-best-other radar PNGs. Caveat from the Tier Standard: LZ4 was timed
  through its command line in this campaign, so slow tools' M is too low; a re-measurement will
  move some tools to slower tiers. Speeds are decode MB/s. Matrix CSV: tier-matrix-2026-11-06.csv.
